Table 1:

Runtime for compositional, local revision and monolithic in seconds, with timeout of 30 minutes.

Evaluation

We evaluated the compositional verification (column “Comp.”) performed via checking GEDC satisfaction (including the verification that it is a GEDC for
\Phi
), and compared that to direct monolithic verification of
\Phi
(column “Mono.”) by constructing the product system.
For the more complex examples, only the compositional algorithm finishes within the time limit,
as the automata grow too large, and are limited by memory.
In the compositional case, we need to handle more but smaller automata.
This addresses the memory consumption but involves more operations.
The overhead causes the monolithic approach to be faster when the specifications are small enough.
Overall, the modular approach scales better.
We report on the verification of local specification and implementation revision (column “Rev.”), which, as expected, is faster than full verification.

Conclusion

We proposed a framework for modular design and analysis of multi-agent systems, based on good-enough decomposition contracts. It offers greater expressivity compared to existing notions of contracts, and we show that it enhances scalability and modularity of verification.
More generally, our approach opens up a new direction in contract-based design of MAS, by extending it to the setting of quantitative specifications and good-enough satisfaction.

Appendix A
Proofs from
Section

4

Theorem 1

(Soundness of GEDC)
.

Let
\Phi
be a compositional LTL
[\mathcal{F}]
specification and
(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
be a GEDC for
\Phi
.
Then, for every MAS
\mathcal{S}=(\mathit{I},\mathit{O},\langle M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}\rangle)
it holds that if
\mathcal{S}\models(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
, then
\mathcal{S}\models\Phi
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{S}=(\mathit{I},\mathit{O},\mathcal{M})
be a MAS that satisfies a GEDC with the conditions of
Definition

5
.
Let
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\in(2^{\mathit{I}})^{\omega}
be an input trace
and let
v\in\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)
be a value such that
there exists
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}}\in(2^{\mathit{O}})^{\omega}
with

\llbracket\Phi,\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}}\rrbracket=v
.
Let us denote

\begin{array}[]{lll}\widetilde{u}_{a}&:=&\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}}\rrbracket$ for all $a\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\\
\widetilde{w}&:=&\llbracket\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}}\rrbracket\end{array}
.

Furthermore, let
\widetilde{\sigma}:=\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}}
.

Let us denote with
\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathit{O}}:=(\parallel_{a=1}^{n}M_{a})(\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}})
the output trace produced by
\mathcal{S}
on
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}
,
and let
\sigma^{\prime}:=\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathit{O}}
.

To show that
\mathcal{S}\models\Phi
, we need to show

\llbracket\Phi,\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket\geq v
.

By the conditions in
Definition

4
and the fact that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\in\mathsf{Hopeful}(v,\Phi)
, we can assume that we have chosen
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}}
such that there exist

(v^{\prime},H,D)\in\mathit{ADec}
and
\widetilde{}\mathbf{d}\in D
where

•

v^{\prime}\geq v,\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\in H
,

\llbracket\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},\widetilde{\sigma}\rrbracket\geq\widetilde{}\mathbf{d}[s]
,

•

\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},\widetilde{\sigma}\rrbracket\geq\widetilde{}\mathbf{d}[a]
for all
a\in[n]
,

•

\widetilde{\sigma}\in\bigcap_{a\in\{1,\ldots,n\}}A_{a}
, and

•

\mathsf{proj}(\widetilde{\sigma},\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}})\in\mathsf{Collab}(D,\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}})_{\bar{a}}
for all
a\in[n]
.

We first establish a helpful property that states that every prefix of
\sigma^{\prime}
has an infinite continuation for which the value of
\Phi
is at least
v^{\prime}
.
This is formalized in the following.

Property 1:

For every
i\in\mathbb{N}
, for the prefix
\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)
of
\sigma^{\prime}
there exist an infinite continuation

\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i}
such that

•

\llbracket\Phi,(\sigma^{\prime}[0,i))\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i}\rrbracket\geq v^{\prime}
,

•

(\sigma^{\prime}[0,i))\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i}\in\bigcap_{a\in\{1,\ldots,n\}}A_{a}
and

•

\mathsf{proj}((\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i}),\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}})\in\mathsf{Collab}(D,\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}})_{\bar{a}}
for all
a\in[n]
.

Proof of Property 1.
The proof is by induction on
i
.

Base case
i=0
.

\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)
is the empty sequence.
Thus,
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{0}:=\widetilde{\sigma}
will have the desired properties by the choice of
\widetilde{\sigma}
.

Induction step.

Suppose that the claim holds for some
i\in\mathbb{N}
, and let us show that it is satisfied by
i+1
as well.
Let us order the agents
1,\ldots,n
.
We will construct
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i+1}
in
n
steps in which we address the agents in the above order.

At each step
a\in\{1,\ldots n\}
we will use the conditions of
Definition

5
applied to agent
a
,
together with the fact that
(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
satisfies the properties of
Definition

4
.
For the corresponding agent
a
we will construct an intermediate trace
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a}
where:

•

\llbracket\Phi,(\sigma^{\prime}[0,i))\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a}\rrbracket\geq v^{\prime}
,

•

(\sigma^{\prime}[0,i))\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a}\in\bigcap_{a\in\{1,\ldots,n\}}A_{a}
,

•

\mathsf{proj}((\sigma^{\prime}[0,i))\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a},\mathit{O}_{\overline{k}})\in\mathsf{Collab}(D,\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}})_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{k}}}
for
k\in[n]
.

•

\mathsf{proj}(\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a},\mathit{O}_{k})[0,i)=M_{k}((\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{k}}})[0,i))
for all agents
k\leq a
.

Once we have constructed
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a}
for
a=n
, we will define
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i+1}:=\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,n}[1,\infty)
, which will satisfy the desired property for index
i+1
by construction.

We now describe the construction of
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a}
.
For convenience, let us define
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,0}:=\widetilde{\sigma}[i,\infty)
.
For
a=1,\ldots,n
we apply conditions
(i)
and
(ii)
from
Definition

5
by instantiating:

•

\sigma_{\mathit{I}}
with
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}
,

•

v
with the value
v^{\prime}
,

•

\sigma_{\mathit{O}}
with
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}}
,

•

\mathbf{d}
with the value decomposition
\widetilde{}\mathbf{d}
,

•

\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}
with
\mathsf{proj}((\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a-1}),\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}})
,

•

\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}
with
\mathsf{proj}((\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a-1}),\mathit{O}_{a})
.

Thus, by condition
(i)
in
Definition

5
we have that

\displaystyle\llbracket\Phi,\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}

\displaystyle\parallel M_{a}\big(\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\mathsf{proj}((\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a-1}),\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}))\big)

\displaystyle\parallel\mathsf{proj}((\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a-1}),\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}})\rrbracket\geq v^{\prime}.

Now, let

\displaystyle\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a}:=\big(\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}

\displaystyle\parallel M_{a}\big(\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\mathsf{proj}((\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a-1}),\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}))\big)

\displaystyle\parallel\mathsf{proj}((\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a-1}),\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}})\big)[i,\infty).

Since
M_{a}
is an implementation of an agent represented as a Moore machine, we have that

M_{a}\big(\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\mathsf{proj}((\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a-1}),\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}))\big)[0,i+1)=\sigma^{\prime}[0,i+1).

Therefore, the above implies that

\llbracket\Phi,((\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathit{O}})[0,i))\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a}\rrbracket\geq v^{\prime}
.
Furthermore, we have that
\mathsf{proj}(\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a},\mathit{O}_{a})[0,i)=M_{a}((\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}})[0,i))
.

What remains is to show that the trace defined in this way satisfies the assumptions.
Since
M_{a}
satisfies condition
(ii)
in
Definition

5
we have that

\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a}\in G_{a}
.
For
k\neq a
we have
\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a-1}\in G_{k}
.
Thus, by the definition of guarantees, we can ensure that by potentially modifying the trace
\mathsf{proj}(\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a},\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}})
we have
that
\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a}\in G_{k}
(since the difference between
\mathsf{proj}(\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a},\mathit{O}_{a})
and
\mathsf{proj}(\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a-1},\mathit{O}_{a})
happens only from position
i
and later,
we can ensure that this does not affect the other desired properties of
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a}
).

Therefore, we have constructed
\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{i,a}
with the desired properties.
This concludes the proof of
Property 1
by induction.

If
\Phi
were a safety LTL
[\mathcal{F}]
specification,
Property 1
would be sufficient to establish the claim of the theorem.
Since this is not the case, we need an additional property pertaining to the local specifications (which are not necessarily safety properties).
We use the additional condition
(iii)
in
Definition

5
to establish this.
It defines the requirements on an implementation for
a
if
\mathsf{proj}(\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathit{O}},\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}})\not\in\mathsf{Collab}(D,\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}})_{\bar{a}}
.

We distinguish two cases.

Case 1:
For some agent
a
, the outputs of the other agents are
D
-collaborative for
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}
and satisfy the assumption
A_{a}
, that is,
\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}\in\mathsf{Collab}(D,\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}})_{\bar{a}}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\in A_{a}
.
In such a case, we can apply condition
(i)
in
Definition

5
to establish that
\llbracket\Phi,\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket=v^{\prime}\geq v
, which concludes the proof in this case.

Case 2:
For every
a
, the outputs of the other agents are either not
D
-collaborative for
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}
or violate the assumption
A_{a}
.
Since
A_{a}
is a safety language, and by
Property 1
every prefix of
\sigma^{\prime}
has a continuation that satisfies all the assumptions, the second case is not possible.
Thus, for every
a
it holds that
\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}\not\in\mathsf{Collab}(D,\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}})_{\bar{a}}
.
Therefore, by condition
(iii)
in
Definition

5
we have that for each
a
,
the trace
\sigma^{\prime}
achieves the maximal possible value for
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}
that is possible for
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}
.
Therefore, for every
a
we have
\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket\geq\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},\widetilde{\sigma}\rrbracket=\widetilde{u}_{a}
.

Assume for the sake of contradiction that
\llbracket\Phi,\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket<v
. This means that
\llbracket\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket<w
.
Since
\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}
is a safety property, there exists an index
i
, such that for every continuation of
\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)
the value of
\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}
is less than
w
.
Further, since
\mathit{Vals}(\Psi_{\mathit{shared}})
is finite, we can choose
i
such that
for every continuation of
\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)
the value of
\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}
is less than or equal to

\llbracket\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
By
Property 1
, there exists a continuation
\sigma_{i}^{\prime\prime}
where the value of
\Phi
is at least
v^{\prime}\geq v
.
By the above, for this continuation, we also have that the value of
\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}
is less than
w
and less than or equal to

\llbracket\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
Thus, we have

\begin{array}[]{lll}\llbracket\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma_{i}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\leq\llbracket\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket&<&w\\
\llbracket\Phi,\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket&<&v\\
\llbracket\Phi,\sigma^{\prime}[0,i)\cdot\sigma_{i}^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket&\geq&v.\end{array}

Thus, by the monotonicity property of
\mathit{comb}
, we have that
for some
a
it holds that
\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},\sigma^{\prime}[0,i).\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket>\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket
, which leads to a contradiction, since
\mathcal{S}
is implemented via Moore machines.
Therefore,
\mathcal{S}
can produce a different output only in the step after that, as input sequences are different.
∎

Theorem 2
.

Let
\Phi
be a compositional LTL
[\mathcal{F}]
specification over atomic propositions
\mathit{AP}=\mathit{I}\uplus\mathit{O}
.
If
\mathcal{S}=(\mathit{I},\mathit{O},\langle M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}\rangle)
is a multi-agent system such that
\mathcal{S}\models\Phi
,
then there exists a good-enough decomposition contract
(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
for
\Phi
such that
\mathcal{S}\models(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{S}=(\mathit{I},\mathit{O},\mathcal{M})
be a multi-agent system that satisfies
Definition

1
for
\Phi
.
For any input trace
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\in(2^{\mathit{I}})^{\omega}
,
\mathcal{S}
produces an output
(\parallel_{a=1}^{n}M_{a})(\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}})=\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}}\in(2^{\mathit{O}})^{\omega}
with some value
\llbracket\Phi,(\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}})\rrbracket=\widetilde{v}
for
\Phi
.
The trace
(\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}})
realizes a specific decomposition
\widetilde{}\mathbf{d}
with
\mathit{comb}(\widetilde{}\mathbf{d})=\widetilde{v}
.
That is,
\widetilde{}\mathbf{d}
is such that for every
a\in[n]
,

\widetilde{}\mathbf{d}[a]=\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}})\rrbracket
, and
\widetilde{}\mathbf{d}[s]=\llbracket\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}})\rrbracket
.

As the number of possible values of
\Phi
and the number of possible decompositions is finite, there is a finite set of decompositions that are used by
\mathcal{S}
for some input sequence.
Therefore we can construct
\mathit{ADec}
by adding for each value decomposition
\mathbf{d}
realized by
\mathcal{S}
for some input sequence the triple
(\mathit{comb}(\mathbf{d}),H_{\mathbf{d}},\{\mathbf{d}\})
, where

H_{\mathbf{d}}=\{\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\in(2^{\mathit{I}})^{\omega}\mid\llbracket(\langle\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}\rangle_{a=1}^{n},\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}),((\parallel_{a=1}^{n}M_{a})(\sigma_{\mathit{I}})\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{I}})\rrbracket\cong\mathbf{d}\}
.

For
\mathit{AG}
we define each
G_{a}^{v}
as the safety language which encodes the implementation
M_{a}
of an agent
a
,
while the assumption
A_{a}^{v}
is the conjunction
\bigwedge_{a^{\prime}\neq a}{G}^{v}_{a^{\prime}}
.
These are the same for all
v
, as they must be sufficient for all input sequences and exactly the allowed decompositions in
\mathit{ADec}
.

\mathit{AG}
therefore precisely captures the composed strategy
(\parallel_{a=1}^{n}M_{a})
.

Now, any input trace
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}
will be in exactly one language
H_{\widetilde{}\mathbf{d}}
for exactly one decomposition
\widetilde{}\mathbf{d}
.
And this is precisely the one obtained by
(\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}})
, where
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{O}}=(\parallel_{a=1}^{n}M_{a})(\widetilde{\sigma}_{\mathit{I}})
.
We combine these to arrive at a good-enough decomposition contract
(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
, which satisfies its conditions (
Definition

4
),
and thus
\mathcal{S}
satisfies the contract according to
Definition

5
.
∎

Appendix B
Details of Verification Methods from
Section

5

Automata Constructions

Automata Representation of Decomposition Contracts

We consider assumptions and guarantees represented by automata.
For each
v\in\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)
we suppose that the assume-guarantee contract
\mathit{AG}(v)=\langle(A_{a}^{v},G_{a}^{v})\rangle_{a=1}^{n}
is represented as follows.
The assumption languages
A_{a}^{v}
, and the complement languages
{(2^{\mathit{AP}})}^{\omega}\setminus G_{a}^{v}
of the guarantees are given respectively as the GNBA with alphabet
2^{\mathit{AP}}
:

\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v}
is the assumption of agent
a
for value
v
.

\overline{\mathcal{G}_{a}^{v}}
is the complement of the guarantee of agent
a
for value
v
.

Furthermore, we assume that for each
(v,H,D)\in\mathit{ADec}
, the language
H
is represented by an GNBA
\mathcal{H}
where
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H})=\{\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}}\in(2^{\mathit{AP}})^{\omega}\mid\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\in H\}
.
We will write
(v,\mathcal{H},D)
instead of
(v,H,D)
for the elements of
\mathit{ADec}
.

Automata for the Specifications

For each of the LTL
[\mathcal{F}]
formulas
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}
,
\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}
and
\Phi
, we can apply the construction from
(
Almagor, Boker, and Kupferman 2016
)
to construct the GNBA

\mathcal{B}_{a}:=\mathcal{A}_{\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},\mathit{Vals}(\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a})}
,

\mathcal{B}_{s}:=\mathcal{A}_{\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},\mathit{Vals}(\Psi_{\mathit{shared}})}
and

\mathcal{B}_{\Phi}:=\mathcal{A}_{\Phi,\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)}
.
Given
\sim\;\in\{<,\geq,=\}
,

u\in\mathit{Vals}(\varphi^{a}_{\mathit{local}})
,

w\in\mathit{Vals}(\Psi_{\mathit{shared}})
and

v\in\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)
,
we obtain automata

\mathcal{B}_{a}^{\sim u}
,
\mathcal{B}_{s}^{\sim w}
,
\mathcal{B}_{\Phi}^{\sim v}
with the following languages:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{B}_{a}^{\sim u})&=&\{\sigma\in(2^{\mathit{AP}})^{\omega}\mid\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},\sigma\rrbracket\sim u\},\\
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{B}_{s}^{\sim w})&=&\{\sigma\in(2^{\mathit{AP}})^{\omega}\mid\llbracket\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},\sigma\rrbracket\sim w\},\\
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{B}_{\Phi}^{\sim v})&=&\{\sigma\in(2^{\mathit{AP}})^{\omega}\mid\llbracket\Phi,\sigma\rrbracket\sim v\}.\end{array}

Proposition 2
.

For each agent
a\in\mathsf{Agt}
, we can construct a GNBA
\mathcal{C}_{a}
such that for any
\sigma=\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}}\in(2^{\mathit{AP}})^{\omega}
we have that

\sigma\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{C}_{a})
if and only if there exists
(v,H,D)\in\mathit{ADec}
such that

\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\in H
, and
\sigma\in A_{a}^{v}
, and at least one of the conditions in
Definition

5
is violated for agent
a
.

Proof.

We construct a GNBA
\mathcal{C}_{a}

that characterizes the language of traces
\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}
such that
there exists
(v,H,D)\in\mathit{ADec}
such that
\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\in H
,
\sigma\in A_{a}^{v}
,
and some of the conditions
(i)
,
(i)
or
(iii)
in
Definition

5
is violated.

First, from the formulas
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}
and
\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}
,
we construct the necessary GNBA

\mathcal{B}_{a}^{\sim u}
and
\mathcal{B}_{s}^{\sim w}
.

Then, for each
(v,\mathcal{H},D)\in\mathit{ADec}
and
\mathbf{d}\in D
, from the GNBA

\mathcal{B}_{a}^{\sim u}
and
\mathcal{B}_{s}^{\sim w}
, and the contract automata
\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v},\overline{\mathcal{G}_{a}^{v}}
and
\mathcal{H}
, we construct the following GNBA:

•

\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}=\mathcal{H}\times\mathsf{proj}_{\exists}(\mathcal{B}_{a}^{=\mathbf{d}[a]}\times\mathcal{B}_{s}^{=\mathbf{d}[s]},\mathit{O}_{a})\times\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v}
,

which captures “good” inputs to agent
a
with respect to
\mathbf{d}
, those that enable
\mathbf{d}
and satisfy the assumption
\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v}
.

•

\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}=\big(\bigcup_{(u,w)\in\mathsf{Bad}(D,\mathbf{d}[\overline{a}])}\mathcal{B}_{a}^{=u}\times\mathcal{B}_{s}^{=w}\big)\cup\overline{\mathcal{G}_{a}}^{v}
,

where
\mathsf{Bad}(D,\mathbf{d}[\overline{a}])\subseteq\mathit{Vals}(\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a})\times\mathit{Vals}(\Psi_{\mathit{shared}})
is such that
(u,w)\in\mathsf{Bad}(D,\mathbf{d}[\overline{a}])
if and only if
for all
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}\in D
,
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}[\overline{a}]\neq\mathbf{d}[\overline{a}]
or
u<\mathbf{d}^{\prime}[a]
or
w<\mathbf{d}^{\prime}[s]
.

This automaton characterizes the “bad” outputs of agent
a
with respect to
\mathbf{d}
,
either by not aligning with
\mathbf{d}
or violating its guarantee
\mathcal{G}_{a}^{v}
.
Here,
\mathsf{Bad}(D,\mathbf{d}[\overline{a}])
enumerates the pairs of values that, from the perspective of
a
, do not conform to
\mathbf{d}
.

•

\mathcal{B}_{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\mathcal{H}\times\overline{\bigcup_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime}\in D}\mathsf{proj}_{\exists}(\mathcal{B}_{a}^{=\mathbf{d}^{\prime}[a]}\times\mathcal{B}_{s}^{=\mathbf{d}^{\prime}[s]},\mathit{O}_{a})}
.

We use this automaton to represent outputs of other agents
\bar{a}
that are not
D
-collaborative for agent
a
, so not in
\mathsf{Collab}(D,\sigma_{\mathit{I}})_{\bar{a}}
,
for inputs
\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\in H
.

•

\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}=\bigcup_{u\in\mathit{Vals}(\varphi_{a})}(\mathsf{proj}_{\exists}(\mathcal{B}_{a}^{=u},\mathit{O}_{a})\times\mathcal{B}_{a}^{<u})
,

characterizing the traces that violate the obligation of best-effort satisfying the local specification
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}
.

Intuitively, the automaton
\bigcup_{\mathbf{d}\in D}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime})
characterizes the language of the traces that violate condition
(i)
or
(ii)
for
(v,H,D)
,
and the automaton
\mathcal{B}_{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}\times\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
the language of the traces that violate condition
(iii)
.

Finally, we construct the GNBA

\mathcal{C}_{a}:=\bigcup_{(v,H,D)\in\mathit{ADec}}\Big(\big(\bigcup_{\mathbf{d}\in D}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime})\big)\cup(\mathcal{B}_{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}\times\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime})\Big).

Note that for a specific agent
a
, the two relevant entries of value decompositions
\mathbf{d}\in D
in terms of its obligations are
\mathbf{d}[a]
and
\mathbf{d}[s]
.
These correspond to the satisfaction value of
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}
and
\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}
, and we can enumerate just the unique pairs
\mathbf{d}[a]
,
\mathbf{d}[s]
for
D
.
The resulting automaton using those pairs only will be equivalent to
\mathcal{C}_{a}
as described above,
as redundant pairs would not introduce any further obligations, and thus not change its language.

For the GNBA
\mathcal{C}_{a}
constructed above, it holds that for
\sigma\in(2^{\mathit{AP}})^{\omega}
we have

\sigma=(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}})\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{C}_{a})
if and only if there exists
(v,H,D)\in\mathit{ADec}
such that

\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\in H
and

\sigma\in A_{a}^{v}
,
and at least one of the following is satisfied.

1.

\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}\in\mathsf{Collab}(v,\sigma_{\mathit{I}})_{\bar{a}}
, and for some
\mathbf{d}\in D

there is an output sequence
\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}^{\prime}\in(2^{\mathit{O}_{a}})^{\omega}
, with

•

\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}^{\prime}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}})\rrbracket=\mathbf{d}[a]
, and

•

\llbracket\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}^{\prime}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}})\rrbracket=\mathbf{d}[s]
.

and for every
\mathbf{d}^{\prime}\in D
it holds that

•

\mathbf{d}^{\prime}[a^{\prime}]\neq\mathbf{d}[a^{\prime}]
for some
a^{\prime}\neq a
, or

•

\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}})\rrbracket<\mathbf{d}^{\prime}[a]
, or

•

\llbracket\Psi_{\mathit{shared}},(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}})\rrbracket<\mathbf{d}^{\prime}[s]
.

2.

\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}\in\mathsf{Collab}(v,\sigma_{I})_{\bar{a}}
,

\sigma\not\in G_{a}^{v}
.

3.

\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}\not\in\mathsf{Collab}(v,\sigma_{I})_{\bar{a}}
and for some
u\in\mathit{Vals}(\varphi_{a})
,
there exists
\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}^{\prime}\in(2^{\mathit{O}_{a}})^{\omega}
such that

\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}^{\prime}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}})\rrbracket=u,

but

\llbracket\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a},(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}})\rrbracket<u
.

The conditions are a direct consequence of the construction of the automata
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime},\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{B}_{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
.

\mathcal{C}_{a}
is the union automaton over all
(v,H,D)\in\mathit{ADec}
,
where either (
1
) for a
\mathbf{d}\in D
the inputs are “good” and the outputs “bad” (
2
),
or (
3
) the outputs of other agents
\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}
are not in
\mathsf{Collab}(v,\sigma_{\mathit{I}})_{\bar{a}}

but outputs of
a

\mathit{O}_{a}
are not “good-enough” with respect to
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}
.
∎

Checking Contract Satisfaction

Complexity Analysis

The initial GNBA constructed from LTL
[\mathcal{F}]
formulas have at most
2^{(l^{2})}
states and at most
l
sets of accepting states, where
l
is the length of the respective formula
(
Almagor, Boker, and Kupferman 2016
)
.
We assume all given automata representations for the contract to be NBA, that is, they have exactly one set of accepting states.

The
size
of an NBA/GNBA
\mathcal{A}
, denoted
|\mathcal{A}|
, is equal to the number of states and transitions in the automaton.

For each
(v,\mathcal{H},D)\in\mathit{ADec}
we establish the following.

•

The automaton

\bigcup_{\mathbf{d}\in D}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime})
has size at most

|\mathit{Vals}(\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a})|\cdot|\mathit{Vals}(\Psi_{\mathit{shared}})|\cdot|\mathcal{H}|\cdot 2^{\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})}\cdot|\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v}|\cdot|\overline{\mathcal{G}_{a}}^{v}|
.
Since the number of possible values is at most exponential in the length of the LTL
[\mathcal{F}]
formula, the size is upper-bounded by

|\mathcal{H}|\cdot 2^{\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})}\cdot|\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v}|\cdot|\overline{\mathcal{G}_{a}}^{v}|
.
In terms of accepting sets,
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}
has at most
|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|+1
,
while
\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime}
has
|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|
.
The union automaton
\bigcup_{\mathbf{d}\in D}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime\prime})
thus has at most
\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|)
sets of accepting states.

•

The automaton
\mathcal{B}_{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}
requires complementation of the projected automata
\mathsf{proj}_{\exists}(\mathcal{B}_{a}^{=\mathbf{d}^{\prime}[a]}\times\mathcal{B}_{s}^{=\mathbf{d}^{\prime}[s]},\mathit{O}_{a})
.
We convert it into an NBA before complementation,
leading to an increase in size by factor
|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|

(
Baier and Katoen 2008
)
, so we size have up to

(|\mathit{Vals}(\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a})|+|\mathit{Vals}(\Psi_{\mathit{shared}})|)\cdot 2^{\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})}
.
The complementation then leads to another exponential blow-up, resulting in size at most

|\mathcal{H}|\cdot 2^{\mathcal{O}((|\mathit{Vals}(\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a})|\cdot|\mathit{Vals}(\Psi_{\mathit{shared}})|\cdot 2^{\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})})\cdot\log b)}
,
where
b=(|\mathit{Vals}(\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a})|\cdot|\mathit{Vals}(\Psi_{\mathit{shared}})|\cdot 2^{\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})})
subsuming the previous increase.
Since the number of possible values is at most exponential in the length of the LTL
[\mathcal{F}]
formula, we obtain the upper bound

|\mathcal{H}|\cdot 2^{2^{\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})}}
.
Because
\mathcal{H}
is also an NBA,
\mathcal{B}_{D}^{\prime\prime\prime}
will only have one set of accepting states.

For agent
a
, the automaton
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
has size upper-bounded by

2^{\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})}
,
and number of sets of accepting states by
|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|
.

Thus, the size of the GNBA
\mathcal{C}_{a}
is at most

2^{2^{\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})}}\cdot\sum_{(v,\mathcal{H},D)\in\mathit{ADec}}|\mathcal{H}|\cdot|\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v}|\cdot|\overline{\mathcal{G}_{a}}^{v}|.

The number of sets of accepting states of
\mathcal{C}_{a}
has an upper bound of
\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|)
.
There exists an NBA equivalent to
\mathcal{C}_{a}
of size

\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{C}_{a}|\cdot|\alpha|)
, where
|\alpha|
is the number of sets of accepting states in
\mathcal{C}_{a}

(
Baier and Katoen 2008
)

Since
|C_{a}|
is already doubly exponential in
|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2}
, the size of the NBA is upper-bounded by

2^{2^{\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})}}\cdot\sum_{(v,\mathcal{H},D)\in\mathit{ADec}}|\mathcal{H}|\cdot|\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v}|\cdot|\overline{\mathcal{G}_{a}}^{v}|.

Checking contract satisfaction can be done by checking language-emptiness on the intersection of the GNBA
\mathcal{C}_{a}
and the respective implementation
M_{a}
for each agent.
This can be done in time linear in the size of the product of
M_{a}
and the non-deterministic Büchi automaton obtained from
\mathcal{C}_{a}

(
Baier and Katoen 2008
;
Vardi and Wolper 1994
)
.
Thus overall we have

\sum_{a\in\mathsf{Agt}}|M_{a}|\cdot 2^{2^{\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})}}\cdot\sum_{(v,\mathcal{H},D)\\
\in\mathit{ADec}}|\mathcal{H}|\cdot|\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v}|\cdot|\overline{\mathcal{G}_{a}}^{v}|.

Theorem 3

(Complexity of Checking Decomposition Contract Satisfaction)
.

Checking if a multi-agent system
\mathcal{S}=(\mathit{I},\mathit{O},\langle M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}\rangle)

satisfies a decomposition contract
(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
for a compositional LTL
[\mathcal{F}]
specification
\Phi

can be done in time linear in

\sum_{a\in\mathsf{Agt}}|M_{a}|\cdot 2^{2^{\mathcal{O}(|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})}}\cdot\sum_{(v,\mathcal{H},D)\\
\in\mathit{ADec}}|\mathcal{H}|\cdot|\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v}|\cdot|\overline{\mathcal{G}_{a}}^{v}|.

Checking Validity of Given Contract

We now describe a procedure for checking whether a given pair
(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
is a good-enough decomposition contract w.r.t. a compositional LTL
[\mathcal{F}]
specification
\Phi
.
This includes checking that
\mathit{ADec}
satisfies the conditions of
Definition

2
, and that for each
v\in\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)
,
\mathit{AG}(v)
satisfies the conditions of
Definition

3
.
This is done as follows:

•

Checking the conditions of
Definition

2
for
\mathit{ADec}
:

(i)

(Input coverage)

Checking that every
v
-hopeful input sequence is covered by some set
H
for value
v^{\prime}\geq v
can be done by verifying for every
v\in\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)
that

\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{proj}_{\exists}(\mathcal{B}_{\Phi}^{=v},\mathit{O}))\subseteq\mathcal{L}(\bigcup_{(v^{\prime},\mathcal{H},D)\in\mathit{ADec}:v^{\prime}\geq v}\mathcal{H})
,
where the GNBA
\mathcal{B}_{\Phi}^{=v}
is constructed from
\Phi
.

(ii)

(Satisfiable decompositions)
can be verified by checking that
\mathcal{L}(\bigtimes_{a=1}^{n}\mathcal{B}_{a}^{=\mathbf{d}[a]}\times\mathcal{B}_{s}^{=\mathbf{d}[s]})\neq\emptyset
for every value decomposition
\mathbf{d}
appearing in
\mathit{ADec}
.

•

Checking the conditions of
Definition

3
for
\mathit{AG}
:

The language inclusion condition can be checked using the respective automata, or ensured by constructing the guarantees based on the assumptions.
The other conditions are ensured by requiring that the assumptions and guarantees are represented by safety automata that syntactically satisfy
(i)
and
(ii)
from
Definition

3
.

•

Checking the conditions of
Definition

4
of GEDC:

The conditions are satisfied if for every
v\in\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)
,
the language
\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{proj}_{\exists}(\mathcal{B}_{\Phi}^{=v},\mathit{O}))

is a subset of

\mathcal{L}(\bigcup_{(v^{\prime},H,D)\in\mathit{ADec}:v^{\prime}\geq v}\bigcup_{\mathbf{d}\in D}(\mathcal{H}\times\mathsf{proj}_{\exists}(\mathcal{E}_{v^{\prime},\mathbf{d}},\mathit{O})))
,
where the automaton
\mathcal{E}_{v^{\prime},\mathbf{d}}
is defined by

\mathcal{E}_{v^{\prime},\mathbf{d}}:=(\bigtimes_{a=1}^{n}\mathcal{B}_{a}^{=\mathbf{d}[a]})\times\mathcal{B}_{s}^{=\mathbf{d}[s]}\times(\bigtimes_{a=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v^{\prime}}).

Complexity Analysis

•

Checking input coverage is performed by checking language inclusion for each
v\in\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)
, that is, by checking emptiness of the language of the product of
\mathsf{proj}_{\exists}(\mathcal{B}_{\Phi}^{=v},\mathit{O})
and

\bigtimes_{(v^{\prime},\mathcal{H},D)\in\mathit{ADec}:v^{\prime}\geq v}\overline{\mathcal{H}}
.
Since
|\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)|
is at most exponential in
|\Phi|
,
the overall time is linear in
2^{\mathcal{O}(|\Phi|^{2})}\cdot\Pi_{{(v^{\prime},\mathcal{H},D)\in\mathit{ADec}:v^{\prime}\geq v}}2^{|\mathcal{H}|}
.

•

Checking the satisfiability of the value decompositions in
\mathit{ADec}
is performed by checking language emptiness for each
\mathbf{d}\in\mathit{ADec}
.
The respective automaton has a size of at most

2^{\mathcal{O}(\sum_{a=1}^{n}|\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}|^{2}+|\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}|^{2})}
,
or
2^{\mathcal{O}(|\Phi|^{2})}
.
Thus, the overall time is linear in
n_{dec}\times 2^{\mathcal{O}(|\Phi|^{2})}
, where
n_{dec}
is the number of value decompositions in
\mathit{ADec}
.

•

Checking the requirements on
\mathit{AG}
is performed by checking language inclusion for each
v\in\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)
and
a\in\mathsf{Agt}
.
Each inclusion check can be done in time exponential in

|\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v}|\cdot\Pi_{a^{\prime}\neq a}|\overline{\mathcal{G}_{a^{\prime}}^{v}}|

(
Sistla, Vardi, and Wolper 1985
)
.
Since
\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)\leq 2^{|\Phi|}
, the total time is bounded by

2^{|\Phi|}\cdot|\mathsf{Agt}|\cdot 2^{|\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v}|\cdot\Pi_{a^{\prime}\neq a}|\overline{\mathcal{G}_{a^{\prime}}^{v}}|}
.

•

Checking the conditions of
Definition

4
is performed by checking language inclusion for each
v\in\mathit{Vals}(\Phi)
.
The GNBA
\mathcal{B}_{\Phi}^{=v}
has at most
2^{|\Phi|^{2}}
states and
|\Phi|
sets of accepting states.
We construct the complement of the automaton

\bigcup_{(v^{\prime},H,D)\in\mathit{ADec}:v^{\prime}\geq v}\bigcup_{\mathbf{d}\in D}(\mathcal{H}\times\mathsf{proj}_{\exists}(\mathcal{E}_{v^{\prime},\mathbf{d}},\mathit{O}))
by complementing
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathsf{proj}_{\exists}(\mathcal{E}_{v^{\prime},\mathbf{d}},\mathit{O})
,
resulting in a complement automaton of size at most
\Pi_{(v^{\prime},H,D)\in\mathit{ADec}:v^{\prime}\geq v}(2^{\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{H}|\cdot\log|H|)}+2^{\mathcal{O}((|D|\cdot 2^{|\Phi|^{2}}\cdot\Pi_{a\in\mathsf{Agt}}|\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v^{\prime}}|)\cdot\log(|D|\cdot 2^{|\Phi|^{2}}\cdot\Pi_{a\in\mathsf{Agt}}|\mathcal{A}_{a}^{v^{\prime}}|))})
.

Modular Verification

Example B.1
.

We consider a case where the requirements are tightened in the design process.
Take the setting from
Example

1.1
, with a contract of
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
forcing alternation every two steps in good weather when
\mathsf{call}
is always true.
The local specification
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{1}
was modified and
a_{1}
must now regularly satisfy
\mathsf{at\_base}_{1}
for
3
turns.
Similar to the case where we loosened requirements, this can affect the obligations in a way opposite to the traditional case, because of the optimality we enforce.
Here, strengthening the requirements makes achieving the previous value impossible for some environment inputs, which excuses the system from stronger obligations for those inputs.

However, an existing implementation will fail since it violates
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{1}
where it previously did not.
The new subformula of
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{1}
reads

Because of the mismatch between the contract and
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{1}
, the previous implementation no longer works.
This is because
a_{1}
is expected to both achieve satisfaction value
1
for
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{1}
and respond to calls after two turns.
Thus, the contract is no longer suitable and requires a revision.

Procedure for Maximizing Local Satisfaction

Consider a compositional specification
\Phi
,
a GEDC
(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
for
\Phi
,
and a MAS
\mathcal{S}=(\mathit{I},\mathit{O},\langle M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}\rangle)
such that
\mathcal{S}\models(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
.
Suppose that the local specification for agent
a
is modified from
{\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}}
to
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{a}
, resulting in a revised global specification
\widehat{\Phi}
.
Let
\widehat{M}_{a}
be a new implementation of agent
a
, and

\widehat{\mathcal{S}}=(\mathit{I},\mathit{O},\langle M_{1},\ldots\widehat{M}_{a},\ldots,M_{n}\rangle)
the resulting MAS.
In order to check whether
\widehat{\mathcal{S}}\models(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
, it suffices to verify only the conditions in
Definition

5
as pertaining to agent
a
,
with respect to the new implementation
\widehat{M}_{a}
of agent
a
and the original local specification
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}
.
If this is the case, by
Theorem

1
, we can conclude that the modified system
\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
still satisfies the original specification
\Phi
.
Otherwise, the new implementation
\widehat{M}_{a}
of agent
a
is incompatible with the given contract.

To further strengthen obligations on agent
a
beyond the existing contract,
we additionally check if the new implementation maximizes the value of
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{a}
while satisfying the existing contract.
That is, whether
\widehat{M}_{a}
satisfies conditions

(i)
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
, and in addition the property below holds:

For all
u\in\mathit{Vals}(\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{a})
, if
\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\in\mathsf{Hopeful}(u,\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{a})
, then it holds that

\llbracket\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{a},(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel M_{a}(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}})\parallel\sigma_{{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}})\rrbracket\geq u
.

The precise property of a new implementation
\widehat{M}_{a}
must then be as follows:
For every input sequence
\sigma_{\mathit{I}}
, output of other components
{\sigma_{{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}}}
, the following conditions are met:

•

If there exists an output
\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}
where

\llbracket\Phi,(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}})\rrbracket=v,

then
\llbracket\Phi,(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}\parallel\widehat{M}_{a}(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}))\rrbracket\geq v
.

•

It here exists an output
\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}}
where

\llbracket{\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{a}}\cap G_{a}^{v},(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{a}})\rrbracket=w,

then
\llbracket{\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{a}},(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}\parallel\widehat{M}_{a}(\sigma_{\mathit{I}}\parallel\sigma_{\mathit{O}_{\overline{a}}}))\rrbracket\geq w
.

This ensures that satisfaction of the modified local specification
\widehat{\varphi}_{\mathit{local}}^{a}
is maximized while the given contract is satisfied.
The extended condition will fail if the new implementation does not satisfy the modified specification or if it violates the contract.

Appendix C
Benchmarks

We implemented our method in a prototype, which takes a compositional
LTL[\mathcal{F}]
specification, an implementation model
\mathcal{S}
and can optionally take a contract
(\mathit{ADec},\mathit{AG})
as input.
The tool will check if the given implementation for each agent satisfies the conditions of contract satisfaction as defined in this paper.
It uses
Spot

(
Duret-Lutz et al. 2022
)
(v2.10.6) for the automata operations.
To our knowledge, there are currently no other tools available that would apply to our setting or could easily be extended to compositional LTL
[\mathcal{F}]
model checking with good-enough satisfaction.

We compare the compositional approach with a monolithic method using our prototype, demonstrating the benefits of compositional methods for the same underlying implementation.
We performed experiments on several examples on a laptop with an Intel Core i7 processor at 2.8 GHz and 16 GB of memory.
We present the results of the evaluation in the main paper.
Below are the examples used in the experimental evaluation, including the contracts used in each case.

delivery_vehicles

The intro example from the paper, a 3-agent-system that has the environment send
\mathsf{call}
and
\mathsf{badw}
via its input atomic proposition.
If there is a
\mathsf{call}
, agents need to respond by moving to
\mathsf{at\_site}_{a}
for the satisfaction of
\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}
.
If there is
\mathsf{badw}
when
a_{1}
or
a_{2}
are traveling, their satisfaction value locally will be reduced.

•

\Phi=\frac{1}{6}\varphi_{1}\oplus\frac{1}{6}\varphi_{2}\oplus\frac{1}{6}\varphi_{3}\oplus\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\mathit{shared}}

•

I=\{\mathsf{call},\mathsf{badw}\}

•

O_{1}=\{\mathsf{at\_base}_{1},\mathsf{at\_site}_{1}\}

•

O_{2}=\{\mathsf{at\_base}_{2},\mathsf{at\_site}_{2}\}

•

O_{3}=\{\mathsf{at\_site}_{3}\}

For
i=1,2
:

\displaystyle\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{i}=

\displaystyle\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.51666pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(\lnot\mathsf{at\_base}_{i}\lor\lnot\mathsf{at\_site}_{i})\land

\displaystyle\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

tasks_collab

A system with 4 agents.
The environment uses the input atomic proposition
\mathsf{task}
to force at least two agents into
\mathsf{joint}
working for the next step.
The best local value for each agent
c
is
\frac{1}{2}
, which is achieved by either doing
\mathsf{work}
or being idle (
\lnot\mathsf{work}_{c}\land\lnot\mathsf{joint}_{c}
).
A decomposition contract is needed to determine which agents sacrifice their local satisfaction to deal with incoming
\mathsf{task}
.
In this case, agents 1 and 2 guarantee to handle
\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}
, s.t. agents 3 and 4 can each satisfy
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{3}
and
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{4}
with the maximum value of
\frac{1}{2}
.
Importantly, if the environment is such that no
\mathsf{task}
is issued, then all agents can focus on their local requirements.

•

\Phi=\frac{1}{6}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{1}\oplus\frac{1}{6}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{2}\oplus\frac{1}{6}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{3}\oplus\frac{1}{6}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{4}\frac{1}{3}\oplus\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}

•

I=\{\mathsf{task}\}

•

O_{c}=\{\mathsf{work}_{c},\mathsf{joint}_{c}\}

•

tasks_scaled

Direct extension to previous example with 6 agents.
The environment uses the input atomic proposition
\mathsf{task}
to force at least two agents into
\mathsf{joint}
working for the next step.
The best local value for each agent is still
\frac{1}{2}
, which is achieved by either doing
\mathsf{work}
or being idle (
\lnot\mathsf{work}_{c}\land\lnot\mathsf{joint}_{c}
).
A decomposition contract is needed to determine which agents devalue their local satisfaction to deal with incoming
\mathsf{task}
.
Here, agents 1 and 3 guarantee to handle
\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}
, s.t. agents 2,4,5,6 can each satisfy
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{a}
with the maximum value of
\frac{1}{2}
.

•

\Phi=\frac{1}{8}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{1}\oplus\frac{1}{8}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{2}\oplus\frac{1}{8}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{3}\oplus\frac{1}{8}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{4}\oplus\frac{1}{8}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{5}\oplus\frac{1}{8}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{6}\frac{1}{4}\oplus\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}

•

I=\{\mathsf{task}\}

•

O_{c}=\{\mathsf{work}_{c},\mathsf{joint}_{c}\}

•

c\in\{1,2,3,4\}

\displaystyle\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{c}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

robots_help_a3

System with 3 agents.
Here, the first agent has a higher weight to its local specification
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{1}
.
Thus the optimal strategy sees agents 2 and 3 sacrificing their satisfaction value to help agent 1 if the environment does not allow for full satisfaction.

robots_help_a5

System with 5 agents, scaled up from previous example.
Here, the first agent again has a higher weight to its local specification
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{1}
.
Thus the optimal strategy sees agents 2 and 3 sacrificing their satisfaction value to help agent 1 if the environment does not allow for full satisfaction.
Agents 4 and 5 are excused by the contract to focus on their own goals.

•

\Phi=({\frac{5}{15}}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{1}\oplus{\frac{1}{15}}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{2}\oplus{\frac{1}{15}}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{3}\oplus{\frac{1}{15}}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{4}\oplus{\frac{1}{15}}\varphi_{\mathit{local}}^{5}\oplus{\frac{6}{15}}\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}

•

I=\{\mathsf{req}\}

•

•

O_{4}=\{\mathsf{help}_{4},\mathsf{proceed}_{4}\}

•

synchr_response

A 3-agent-system that needs to respond to environment input atomic proposition
\mathsf{call}
by
\mathsf{respond}
at the same time.
Each agent needs to
\mathsf{work}
, where different lengths of consecutive work change the satisfaction of
\varphi_{\mathit{local}}
.
If no
\mathsf{call}
comes in, the best value overall is
1
, and each agent can either
\mathsf{work}
, or
\mathsf{respond}
.
Note that each agent is free to skip every other work, as the overall value is the minimum over all subformulas.

•

\Phi=\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}\land\varphi_{3}\land\Psi_{\mathit{shared}}

•

I=\{\mathsf{call}\}

•

O_{c}=\{\mathsf{work}_{c},\mathsf{respond}_{c}\}
